T^{\prime\prime}\to\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\beta}(K)
in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
and it will be zero. Then we will have a commutative diagram in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
as follows:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{\beta}(G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\beta}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}(x)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Notice that
T^{\prime\prime}\to\mathbb{C}(x)
should be an injection in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
by the property of pullback. Then
T^{\prime\prime}=0
or
T^{\prime\prime}\cong\mathbb{C}(x)
. Moreover, by the snake lemma, we have a short exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{\beta}(G)\to i_{*}\mathcal{E}^{\oplus r^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathrm{coker}(T^{\prime\prime}\to\mathbb{C}(x))\to 0
in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
.

If
T^{\prime\prime}\cong\mathbb{C}(x)
, then
\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{\beta}(G)\cong i_{*}\mathcal{E}^{\oplus r^{\prime\prime}}
and it implies that
\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{\beta}(G)=0
and
r^{\prime\prime}=0
. In this case,
G=0
.

If
T^{\prime\prime}=0
and we assume that
r^{\prime\prime}>0
, by taking
\mathrm{Hom}(i_{*}\mathcal{E},-)
functor on

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{\beta}(G)\to i_{*}\mathcal{E}^{\oplus r^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathbb{C}(x)\to 0,

we could know that
r^{\prime\prime}\leqslant r
. On the other hand, we have a commutative diagram in
\mathcal{I}_{0}^{\beta,i_{*}\mathcal{E}}(X)
as follows:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i_{*}\mathcal{E}^{\oplus r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}(x)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}^{x}[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{\beta}(G)[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{-1}_{\beta}(G)[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

By the snake lemma, we have a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{I}_{0}^{\beta,i_{*}\mathcal{E}}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to i_{*}\mathcal{E}^{\oplus r}\to i_{*}\mathcal{E}^{\oplus r^{\prime\prime}}\to F\to 0.

It implies that
r^{\prime\prime}=r
and thus
F=0
. In conclusion,
\mathcal{E}^{x}[1]\in\mathcal{I}_{0}^{\beta,i_{*}\mathcal{E}}
is a simple object.
∎

Lemma 5.21
.

[
BM11
, Lemma 5.9]

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a triangulated category and
v\colon K(\mathcal{D})\to\lambda
be a group homomorphism and
E\in\mathcal{D}
. Suppose that
\sigma\in\mathrm{Stab}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D})
is a stability condition such that
E
is
\sigma
-semistable and if:

(1) there is a Jordan-Hölder filtration

\displaystyle 0\to F^{\oplus r}\to E\to G\to 0

of
E
such that
F,G
are
\sigma
-stable;

(2)
\mathrm{Hom}(E,F)=0
;

(3)
v(E)
and
v(F)
are linearly independant in
\Lambda
.

Then
\sigma
is in the closure of the set of stability conditions where
E
is stable.

Corollary 5.22
.

Let the hypotheses be as in Proposition
5.13
. Then the stability condition
\sigma^{\beta,i_{*}\mathcal{E},a}=(Z^{\beta,\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}^{\beta},a},\mathrm{Coh}^{\beta,i_{*}\mathcal{E}}_{0}(X))
is on the boundary of the set of geometric stability conditions. In particular, if
-\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}<\beta<0
is a rational number and
\displaystyle\frac{3\beta^{3}+6\beta^{2}-4}{6(3\beta+2)}<a<\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\beta^{2}
is a real number, then
(Z^{\beta,\beta^{2},a},\mathrm{Coh}^{\beta,i_{*}\mathcal{O}}_{0}(X))\in\partial\mathrm{Stab}_{H}^{\mathrm{geo}}(D_{0}^{b}(X))
.

Proof.

Notice that we have a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{I}_{0}^{\beta,i_{*}\mathcal{E}}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to i_{*}\mathcal{E}^{\oplus r}\to\mathbb{C}(x)\to\mathcal{E}^{x}[1]\to 0.

And
i_{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}^{x}[1]
are both
\sigma^{\beta,i_{*}\mathcal{E},a}
-stable by Proposition
5.19
and
5.20
. Therefore, by Lemma
[
BM11
, Lemma 5.9]
above, we get our conclusion.
∎

Finally, we have:

Proposition 5.23
.

If
-\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}<\beta<0
is a rational number and
\displaystyle\frac{3\beta^{3}+6\beta^{2}-4}{6(3\beta+2)}<a<\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\beta^{2}
is a real number, then
\sigma^{\beta,i_{*}\mathcal{O},a}=(Z^{\beta,\beta^{2},a},\mathrm{Coh}^{\beta,i_{*}\mathcal{O}}_{0}(X))\in\overline{\mathrm{Stab}_{H}^{\mathrm{geo}}(D_{0}^{b}(X))}\cap\overline{\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}(\mathrm{Stab}_{H}^{\mathrm{geo}}(D_{0}^{b}(X))})
.

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{C}(x))\cong\mathrm{Cone}(\mathrm{RHom}_{D_{0}^{b}(X)}(i_{*}\mathcal{O},\mathbb{C}(x))\otimes i_{*}\mathcal{O}\to\mathbb{C}(x))

But
\mathrm{RHom}_{D_{0}^{b}(X)}(i_{*}\mathcal{O},\mathbb{C}(x))\cong\bigoplus_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{0}^{b}(X)}(i_{*}\mathcal{O},\mathbb{C}(x)[l])[-l]\cong\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}[-1]
and we have an exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{H}^{-1}(\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{C}(x)))\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)}(i_{*}\mathcal{O},\mathbb{C}(x))\otimes i_{*}\mathcal{O}\to\mathbb{C}(x)\to\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{C}(x)))\to

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)}(i_{*}\mathcal{O},\mathbb{C}(x)[1])\otimes i_{*}\mathcal{O}\to 0.

Thus
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{C}(x)))\cong\mathcal{O}^{x}
and
\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{C}(x)))\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)}(i_{*}\mathcal{O},\mathbb{C}(x)[1])\otimes i_{*}\mathcal{O}\to 0\cong i_{*}\mathcal{O}
by the definition of
\mathcal{O}^{x}
. Then we have a distinguished triangle:

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{x}[1]\to\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{C}(x))\to i_{*}\mathcal{O}\to\mathcal{O}^{x}[2].

Take the inverse of
\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}
on the above triangle:

\displaystyle\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}^{x}[1])\to\mathbb{C}(x)\to\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}^{-1}(i_{*}\mathcal{O})\to\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}^{x}[2]).

Note that:
\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}(i_{*}\mathcal{O})=i_{*}\mathcal{O}[-3]
, thus:

\displaystyle\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}^{x}[1])\to\mathbb{C}(x)\to i_{*}\mathcal{O}[3]\to\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}^{x}[2]).

Let
\eta^{\beta,i_{*}\mathcal{O},a}=\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}^{-1}\cdot\sigma^{\beta,i_{*}\mathcal{O},a}\in\mathrm{Stab}_{H}(D_{0}^{b}(X))
, then
\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}^{x}[1])
and
i_{*}\mathcal{O}[3]
are
\eta^{\beta,i_{*}\mathcal{O},a}
-stable object. By
[
BM11
, Lemma 5.9]
, we have
\eta^{\beta,i_{*}\mathcal{O},a}\in\partial\mathrm{Stab}_{H}^{\mathrm{geo}}(D_{0}^{b}(X))
, thus
\sigma^{\beta,i_{*}\mathcal{O},a}\in\overline{\mathrm{Stab}_{H}^{\mathrm{geo}}(D_{0}^{b}(X))}\cap\overline{\mathrm{ST}_{i_{*}\mathcal{O}}(\mathrm{Stab}_{H}^{\mathrm{geo}}(D_{0}^{b}(X))})
.
∎

Appendix A
Relative derived dual functor

In this appendix, we introduce the notion and some properties of relative derived dual functor.

A.1.
Basic definitions and properties

First, for any smooth quasi-projective variety
W
over
\mathbb{C}
with dimension
n
, we define its
derived dual functor
as usual:

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{W}\triangleq R\mathcal{H}om(-,\mathcal{O}_{W})\colon D^{b}(W)\to D^{b}(W).

Note that we have the following basic proposition of this functor and we can find the proof in
[
HL10
, Section1.1]
written by Huybrechts and Lehn:

(1) We have
\mathbb{D}_{W}\circ\mathbb{D}_{W}\cong 1_{D^{b}(W)}
, where
1_{D^{b}(W)}
means the identity functor of
D^{b}(W)
.

(2) Let
E
be a nonzero coherent sheaf on
W
and the dimension of
E
is
d
, then we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{W}(E))=\mathcal{E}xt^{l}_{W}(E,\mathcal{O}_{W})=0

unless
l\in\{n-d,\ldots,n\}
. And the dimension of the sheaf
\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{W}(E))
is less than or equal to
n-l
when
l\in\{n-d,\ldots,n\}
.

Moreover, if
E
is a pure sheaf of dimension
d
, then:

(1) For any
l\notin\{n-d,\ldots,n-1\}
, we have
\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{W}(E))=0
.

(2) The dimension of sheaf
\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{W}(E))
is less than or equal to
n-l-1
when
l\in\{n-d+1,\ldots,n-1\}
, and the sheaf
\mathcal{H}^{n-d}(\mathbb{D}_{W}(E))
is pure of dimension
d
with the same support set of
E
. Moreover, if
E
is just a nonzero coherent sheaf on
W
with dimension
d
, then
\mathcal{H}^{n-d}(E)
is still pure of dimension
d
.

(3) If
W
is projective, then we have:

\displaystyle\mathrm{ch}_{i}(\mathbb{D}_{W}(E))=(-1)^{i}\mathrm{ch}_{i}(E)

for any
i\in\{0,\ldots,n\}
and
E\in\mathrm{Coh}(W)
.

Now we introduce the notation of relative derived dual functor. We still use the notion we have used in the main body of this paper. Explicitly, suppose that
Y
is a smooth projective variety over
\mathbb{C}
with dimension
n
and
X=\mathrm{Tot}_{Y}(\mathcal{E}_{0})
is the total space of a locally free sheaf
\mathcal{E}_{0}
with constant rank
r
on
Y
. Let
\pi\colon X\to Y
be the natural projection and
i\colon Y\to X
be the zero section of
\pi
. We always fix a polarization
H
of
Y
and use
v_{i}(E)
to represent the number

H^{n-i}\mathrm{ch}_{i}(\pi_{*}E)
for any
E\in D_{0}^{b}(X)
.

We define the relative derived dual functor with respect to
\pi\colon X\to Y
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}\triangleq R\mathcal{H}om(-,\pi^{*}\mathrm{det}\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\lor}[r])\colon D^{b}_{0}(X)\to D^{b}_{0}(X).

One can quickly check that
\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}\circ\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}\cong 1_{D^{b}_{0}(X)}
and
\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}
is the composition of
\mathbb{D}_{X}
and tensoring with the object
\pi^{*}\mathrm{det}\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\lor}[r]
.

Proposition A.1
.

For any
E\in D_{0}^{b}(X)
, we have

\displaystyle\pi_{*}\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)\cong\mathbb{D}_{Y}(\pi_{*}E).

in
D^{b}(Y)
. Thus, for any
i\in\{0,\ldots,n\}
,
v_{i}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=(-1)^{i}v_{i}(E)
.

Proof.

We conclude this proposition directly by the Grothendieck-Verdier duality.
∎

If
E\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
is a sheaf with dimension
d
, then the sheaf
\mathcal{H}^{n-d}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
is a pure sheaf of dimension
d
. We call the sheaf
\mathcal{H}^{n-d}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))
as the relative dual sheaf of
E
and still denote it by
E^{\lor}
. We may get confused about whether the notion
E^{\lor}
represents
\mathcal{H}^{n-d}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))
or
\mathcal{H}^{n+r-d}(\mathbb{D}_{X}(E))
. But in this appendix, without special statement,
E^{\lor}
always represents
\mathcal{H}^{n-d}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))
where
E\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
.

Before our further discussion, there is a useful lemma about Bridgeland stability condition:

Lemma A.2
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a triangulated category and
\sigma=(Z,\mathcal{A})
be a weak pre-stability condition on
\mathcal{D}
with slope function
\mu
. Suppose that there is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle 0\to F\to E\to G\to 0,

where
F,E\neq 0
and
Z(G)=0
.

(1) We have
\mu_{\min}(F)=\mu_{\min}(E)
, and if
\mathrm{Hom}(C,E)=0
for any
C\in\mathcal{A}
with
Z(C)=0
, then
\mu_{\max}(F)=\mu_{\max}(E)
.

(2)
[
PT19
, Sublemma 2.16]
If
F
is
\sigma
-semistable and
\mathrm{Hom}(C,E)=0
for any
C\in\mathcal{A}
with
Z(C)=0
, then
E
is
\sigma
-semistable with slope
\mu(F)
.

Proof.

(1) We do induction on the length
m
of Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
E
.

If
m=1
, it means that
E
is
\sigma
-semistable. For any nontrivial subobject
F^{\prime}
of
F
, it is also a subobject of
E
, then
\mu(F^{\prime})\leqslant\mu(E)
. Meanwhile,
Z(G)=0
implies that
\mu(E)=\mu(F)
, then
\mu(F^{\prime})\leqslant\mu(F)
. Thus,
F
is
\sigma
-semistable with slope
\mu(E)
.

Assume that our conclusion holds if
m<k
. When
m=k>1
, we consider the Harder-Narasimhan factor
E_{k}
of
E
with the minimum slope. Let
f
be the composition of
F\to E\to E_{k}
, then there will be a commutative diagram in
\mathcal{A}
with two rows are both exact:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Im}f\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{k}/\operatorname{Im}f\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

By the snake lemma, the morpshism
G\to E_{k}/\operatorname{Im}f
is an epimorphism in
\mathcal{A}
and then
Z(E_{k}/\operatorname{Im}f)=0
. Note that
\mathrm{Im}f\neq 0
, otherwise, we will have
Z(E_{k})=0
and it is a contradiction. Thus by our proof in the first paragraph, we have
\operatorname{Im}f
is also
\sigma
-semistable with
\mu_{\min}(E)=\mu(\mathrm{Im}f)
.

On the other hand, we denote the kernel of
E\to E_{k}
by
E^{\prime}
, the kernel of
F\to\operatorname{Im}f
by
F^{\prime}
and the kernel of
G\to E_{n}/\operatorname{Im}f
by
G^{\prime}
, then
0\to F^{\prime}\to E^{\prime}\to G^{\prime}\to 0
is an exact sequence in
\mathcal{A}
with
Z(G^{\prime})=0
by the snake lemma. If
F^{\prime}=0
, then
\mu_{\min}(F)=\mu(F)=\mu(\mathrm{Im}f)=\mu_{\min}(E)
. If
F^{\prime}\neq 0
, then by the assumption of induction, we have
\mu_{\min}(F^{\prime})=\mu_{\min}(E^{\prime})>\mu(E_{n})=\mu(\operatorname{Im}f)
, thus
\mu_{\min}(F)=\mu(\operatorname{Im}f)=\mu_{\min}(E)
.

Moreover, if
\mathrm{Hom}(C,E)=0
for any
C\in\mathcal{A}
with
Z(C)=0
,
F^{\prime}
will not be
0
, because it implies
Z(E^{\prime})=0
and it is a contradiction. Meanwhile, we conclude that
\mathrm{Hom}(C,E^{\prime})=0
for any
C\in\mathcal{A}
with
Z(C)=0
and by our assumption,
\mu_{\max}(F^{\prime})=\mu_{\max}(E^{\prime})
.
Thus
\mu_{\max}(F)=\mu_{\max}(E)
.

(2) It is a direct corollary of (1).
∎

Proposition A.3
.

Suppose that and
E\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
is pure of dimension
n
, then:

(1) We have a short exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to E\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{E}}}{{\longrightarrow}}E^{\lor\lor}\to E^{\lor\lor}/E\to 0,

where
E^{\lor\lor}/E
is
0
or the dimension of
E^{\lor\lor}/E
is less than or equal to
n-2
. In other words,
v_{0}(E^{\lor\lor}/E)=v_{1}(E^{\lor\lor}/E)=0
.

(2) If
E
is slope semistable, then
E^{\lor\lor}
is also slope semistable with the same slope.

(3) If
E
is slope semistable, then
E^{\lor}
is also slope semistable with slope
-\mu(E)
.

(4) We always have
\mu_{\min}(E^{\lor})=-\mu_{\max}(E)
and
\mu_{\max}(E^{\lor})=-\mu_{\min}(E)
.

(5) Moreover, suppose that there is a short exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\longrightarrow}}E\to G\to 0

with
F\neq 0
and
G
is
0
or the dimension of
G
is less that or equal to
n-2
. Then there is a commutative diagram in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{F}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{F^{\lor\lor}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\lor\lor}}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{E}}
\textstyle{E^{\lor\lor}}

with
f^{\lor\lor}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

(1) Because
\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=0
for any
l<0
, then we have a natural morphism:
\phi\colon E^{\lor}\to\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)
, such that
\mathcal{H}^{0}(\phi)
is an isomorphism. Consider
E^{\prime}
as the cone of this morphism, then we have a distinguished triangle in
D^{b}_{0}(X)
:

(7)

\displaystyle E^{\lor}\to\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)\to E^{\prime}\to E^{\lor}[1].

After taking cohomology and note that
\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=0
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})\cong\begin{cases}0&l\leqslant 0\mbox{ or }l\geqslant n;\\
\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))&0<l<n.\end{cases}

Thus, when
0<l<n
, then
\dim\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})=\dim\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=\dim\mathcal{H}^{r+l}(\mathbb{D}_{X}(E))\leqslant n-l-1
.

We take the relatived dual functor of the sequence (
7
) and we have a distinguished triangle in
D_{0}^{b}(X)
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E^{\prime})\to E\to\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E^{\lor})\to\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E^{\prime})[1].

After taking cohomology with respect to the heart
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
, we have an exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E^{\prime}))\to E\to E^{\lor\lor}\to\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E^{\prime}))\to 0

Finally, we just need to claim that
\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E^{\prime}))=0
and
\dim\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E^{\prime}))\leqslant n-2
.

If
n=1
, then we find that
E^{\prime}=0
and then
E\cong E^{\lor\lor}
. If
n\geqslant 2
, we consider a filtration of
E^{\prime}
as follows:

\textstyle{0=E_{0}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{1}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{n-2}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{n-1}^{\prime}=E^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{1}(E^{\prime})[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(E^{\prime})[1-n]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

In other words, there is a series of distinguished triangle here:
E_{l-1}^{\prime}\to E_{l}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})[-l]\to E_{l-1}[1]
for any
l\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
. After taking the relatived dual functor, we have a series of distinguished triangle in
D_{0}^{b}(X)
:
\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime}))[l]\to\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E_{l}^{\prime})\to\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E_{l-1}^{\prime})\to\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime}))[l+1]
. After taking cohomology with respect to the heart
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
, we have an exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle\cdots\to

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})))\to\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E_{l}^{\prime}))\to\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E_{l-1}^{\prime}))\to

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{l+1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})))\to\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E_{l}^{\prime}))\to\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E_{l-1}^{\prime}))\to\cdots.

Note that we only need to prove that:
\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})))=0
and
\dim\mathcal{H}^{l+1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})))\leqslant n-2
for any
l\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
.

In fact,
\dim\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})\leqslant n-1-l
, then
l<n-\dim\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})
. It means
\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})))=0
. Meanwhile, if
\dim\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})<n-1-l
, then
\mathcal{H}^{l+1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})))=0
for the same reason. If
\dim\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})=n-1-l
, then
\dim\mathcal{H}^{l+1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})))\leqslant\dim\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})=n-1-l\leqslant n-2
. And we finish the proof.

(2) It is a direct conclusion of Lemma
A.2
and (1).

(3) First, note that
(-1)^{i}v_{i}(E)=v_{i}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=v_{i}(E^{\lor})+v_{i}(E^{\prime})
. And when
i=0
or
1
, we have
v_{i}(E^{\prime})=\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}(-1)^{l}v_{i}(\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime}))=0
because
\dim\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})\leqslant n-1-l\leqslant n-2
for any
l\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
. Thus,
v_{0}(E^{\lor})=v_{0}(E)
,
v_{1}(E^{\lor})=-v_{1}(E)
and
\mu(E^{\lor})=-\mu(E)
. Moreover, if
H\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
is a nonzero sheaf with dimension
n
, then there is a torsion decomposition in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to H_{t}\to H\to H_{tf}\to 0

where
H_{tf}\neq 0
is pure of dimension
n
and
\dim H_{t}\leqslant n-1
. It means that:

\displaystyle\mu(H^{\lor})=\mu(H_{tf}^{\lor})=-\mu(H_{tf})=-\frac{v_{1}(H_{tf})}{v_{0}(H_{tf})}=-\frac{v_{1}(H)-v_{1}(H_{t})}{v_{0}(H)}\geqslant-\mu(H).

Now, suppose that
E^{\lor}
is not slope semistable, then there is a short exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to A\to E^{\lor}\to B\to 0

with
\mu(A)>\mu(E^{\lor})\geqslant\mu(B)
and
A,B\neq 0
. Because
E^{\lor}
is pure of dimension
n
, then
A
is also pure of dimension
n
and
+\infty>\mu(A)>\mu(E^{\lor})>\mu(B)
. After taking the relative dual functor and the cohomology functor, we have an exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to B^{\lor}\to E^{\lor\lor}\to A^{\lor}

Now
E^{\lor\lor}
is
\sigma
-semistable by (2) and
B^{\lor}
is a nonzero subsheaf of
E^{\lor\lor}
. Thus

\displaystyle-\mu(B)\leqslant\mu(B^{\lor})\leqslant\mu(E^{\lor\lor})=\mu(E).

It is a contradiction.

(4) We do induction on the length
m
of Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
E
. If
m=1
, then it is the case in (3). Assume that our conclusion holds if
m<k
. When
m=k>1
, we consider the Harder-Narasimhan factor
E_{k}
of
E
with the minimum slope and then we have a short exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to E^{\prime}\to E\to E_{k}\to 0

After taking relative dual functor and the cohomology functor, we have an exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to E_{k}^{\lor}\to E^{\lor}\to E^{\prime\lor}\to\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E_{k})).

Divide it into two short exact sequences:

\displaystyle 0\to E_{k}^{\lor}\to E^{\lor}\to H\to 0

\displaystyle 0\to H\to E^{\prime\lor}\to H^{\prime}\to 0,

where
H^{\prime}
is a subsheaf of
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E_{k}))
.

Note that
\dim H^{\prime}\leqslant n-2
and by Lemma
A.2
, we have
\mu_{\min}(H)=\mu_{\min}(E^{\prime\lor})
and
\mu_{\max}(H)=\mu_{\max}(E^{\prime\lor})
. By our assumption of induction, there are
\mu_{\min}(E^{\prime\lor})=-\mu_{\max}(E^{\prime})
and
\mu_{\max}(E^{\prime\lor})=-\mu_{\min}(E^{\prime})
. Meanwhile, we have
\mu(E_{k}^{\lor})=-\mu(E_{k})>-\mu_{\min}(E^{\prime})=\mu_{\max}(H)
. It means that
\mu_{\max}(E^{\lor})=\mu(E_{k}^{\lor})=-\mu(E_{k})=-\mu_{\min}(E)
and
\mu_{\min}(E^{\lor})=\mu_{\min}(H)=\mu_{\min}(E^{\prime\lor})=-\mu_{\max}(E^{\prime})=-\mu_{\max}(E)
. Thus we get our conclusion.

(5) It is not hard to see that
\iota_{E}\circ f=f^{\lor\lor}\circ\iota_{F}
. By taking the functor
\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(-))
, we know that
\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(G))=0
. It implies
f^{\lor}
is an isomorphism and
f^{\lor\lor}
is also an isomorphism.
∎

Remark A.4
.

For any pure sheaf
E
, if the morphism
E\to E^{\lor\lor}
which we construct in the above proposition is an isomorphism, then we call that
E
is reflexive. In fact, if
E
is reflexive with dimension
d
, then the dimension of the sheaf
\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))
is less than or equal to
n-l-2
when
l\in\{n-d+1,\ldots,n-2\}
. The proof is the same as the proof in
[
HL10
]
.

In fact, there is a similar proposition in the first tilting herat
\mathrm{Coh}^{\beta}_{0}(X)
for any
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
when
n=2,3
.

Definition A.5
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and
0\neq E\in\mathrm{Coh}^{\beta}_{0}(X)
. If
v_{1}^{\beta}(H)>0
for any nonzero subobject
H
of
E
in
\mathrm{Coh}^{\beta}_{0}(X)
, then we call
E
is tilt-torsionfree.

Lemma A.6
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and
0\neq E\in\mathrm{Coh}^{\beta}_{0}(X)
, then for any
l<0
and
l>n
, we have
\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=0
. Moreover, for any
l\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
, we have
\dim\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\leqslant n-l
.

Proof.

We denote
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(E)
by
F
and
\mathcal{H}^{0}(E)
by
T
. Note that if
F\neq 0
then it must be a pure sheaf of dimension
n
.

We have a short exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
as follows:

\displaystyle 0\to F[1]\to E\to T\to 0.

Then we have a distinguished triangle in
D_{0}^{b}(X)
after taking the relative dual functor:

(8)

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(T)\to\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)\to\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(F)[-1]\to\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(T)[1].

By taking cohomology functor on the above distinguished triangle (
8
) with respect to the heart
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
, we have a long exact sequence:

\displaystyle\cdots\to\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(T))\to\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\to\mathcal{H}^{l-1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(F))\to\cdots

Now, if
l<0
or
l>n
, then
\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(T))=0
and
\mathcal{H}^{l-1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(F))=0
; Moreover, if
l\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
, then
\dim\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(T))\leqslant n-l
and
\dim\mathcal{H}^{l-1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(F))\leqslant n-l
. And then
\dim\mathcal{H}^{l-1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\leqslant n-l

∎

Lemma A.7
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and suppose that
0\neq E\in\mathrm{Coh}^{\beta}_{0}(X)
is tilt-torsionfree, then:

(1) The sheaf
\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\in\mathrm{Coh}^{<-\beta}_{0}(X)
and
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\in\mathrm{Coh}^{>-\beta}_{0}(X)
.

(2) The sheaf
\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=0
.

(3) When
n\geqslant 3
, we have
\dim\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=0
.

Proof.

We denote
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(E)
by
F
and
\mathcal{H}^{0}(E)
by
T
.

(1) We still take the cohomology functor on (
8
). Then we get
\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(T))\cong\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))
and an exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

(9)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(T))\to\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\to\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(F))\to\mathcal{H}^{2}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(T)).

If
T=0
or
\dim T<n
, then
\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(T))=0
and it means that
\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\in\mathrm{Coh}^{\leqslant-\beta}_{0}(X)
. Otherwise, if
\dim T=n
, then there is a short exact sequence of
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to T_{t}\to T\to T_{tf}\to 0,

where
T_{tf}\neq 0
is a pure sheaf of dimension
n
and
\dim T_{t}\leqslant n-1
. Then
\mu_{\min}(T_{tf})=\mu_{\min}(T)>\beta
and by Proposition
A.3
, we have:

\displaystyle\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)))=\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(T)))=\mu_{\max}(T_{tf}^{\lor})=-\mu_{\min}(T_{tf})<-\beta.

On the other hand, we split the exact sequence (
9
) into two short exact sequneces as follows:

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(T))\to\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\to H_{1}\to 0.

\displaystyle 0\to H_{1}\to\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(F))\to H_{2}\to 0,

where
H_{2}
is a subsheaf of
\mathcal{H}^{2}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(T))
and
\dim H_{2}\leqslant n-2
if it is nonzero. By Lemma
A.2
, we have:

\displaystyle\mu_{\min}(H_{1})=\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(F)))=-\mu_{\max}(F)\geqslant-\beta

if
F
is nonzero. Moreover, we have
\mu_{\max}(F)<\beta
, otherwise, there is a subobject
F_{\max}
of
F
which has slope
\beta
and
F_{\max}[1]
is also a nonzero subobject of
E
. However,
v_{1}^{\beta}(F[1])=0
, it is a contradiction. Thus
H_{1}\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{>-\beta}(X)
. On the other hand,
\dim\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(T))\leqslant n-1
if it is nonzero, thus
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(T))\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{>-\beta}(X)
. Therefore,
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{>-\beta}(X)
.

(2) If
\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\neq 0
, then it should be a zero dimensional sheaf. Now we have a nonzero morphism
\phi\colon\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)\to\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))[-n]
and we can take the relative derived dual functor of it to get a nonzero morphism
\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(\phi)\colon A\to E
for some zero dimensional sheaf
A
. However, we know that
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{0}^{b}(X)}(A,E)=0
because
E
is tilt-torsionfree.

(3) By Lemma
A.6
, we know that
\dim\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\leqslant 1
. If
\dim\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=1
, then we denote
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))
by
B_{1}
. Note that we always have an epimorphism
\phi_{1}\colon B_{1}\to B_{2}
, where
B_{2}
is a pure sheaf of dimension one. Now we have a nonzero morphism
\phi_{2}\colon\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)\to B_{1}[-n+1]
, where
H^{n-1}(\phi_{2})
is an isomorphism. Therefore,
\phi_{1}[-n+1]\circ\phi_{2}
is a nonzero morphism from
\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)
to
B_{2}[-n+1]
. Thus
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{0}^{b}(X)}(B_{3},E)=\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{0}^{b}(X)}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E),B_{2}[-n+1])\neq 0
, where
B_{3}=\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(B_{2}))
is a pure sheaf of dimension one. But it is a contradiction because
E
is tilt-torsionfree.
∎

Note that for any object
0\neq E\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
, we have a filtration of
\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)
in
D_{0}^{b}(X)
:

\textstyle{0=E_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{n-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{n}=\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))[-n+1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

and there is a distinguished triangle in
D_{0}^{b}(X)
here:

\displaystyle E_{2}\to\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)\to E_{2}^{\prime}\to E_{2}[1].

Here,
E_{2}
is a two-term complex with the distinguished triangle:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\to E_{2}\to\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))[-1]\to\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))[1]

If
E
is tilt-torsionfree, then by Lemma
A.7
, we know that
E_{2}[1]\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)
. We define
E_{2}[1]
as the tilt-dual object of
E
and denote it by
E^{\lor_{\beta}}
. In fact, by Lemma
A.6
, for any
l\in\{2,\ldots,n-1\}
, we have
\dim\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\leqslant n-l\leqslant n-2
. It means that
\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)
and the filtration (
A.1
) is a Harder-Narasimhan filtration with respect to the bounded heart
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)
if we consider that
E_{2}
is a object in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)[-1]
. Explicitly, we have:

(14)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{l}_{-\beta}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\cong\begin{cases}0&l\leqslant 0\mbox{ or }l\geqslant n;\\
E^{\lor_{\beta}}&l=1;\\
\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))&1<l<n,\end{cases}

where
\mathcal{H}^{l}_{-\beta}
is the cohomology functor with respect to the bounded heart
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)
.

Before our discussion, we have an important lemma:

Lemma A.8
.

If
E\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
with
v_{1}^{\beta}(E)=0
. Then for any
l\leqslant 1
, we have
\mathcal{H}^{l}_{-\beta}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=0
.

Proof.

We denote
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(E)
by
F
and
\mathcal{H}^{0}(E)
by
T
.

Because
v_{1}^{\beta}(E)=0
, we know that
\dim T\leqslant n-2
or
T=0
and
F
is slope semistable with slope
\beta
or
F=0
. Thus, after taking the cohomology functor of (
8
), we have:
\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=0
and an exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\to\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(F))\to\mathcal{H}^{2}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(T)).

If
F=0
, it is trivial. If
F\neq 0
, then
F^{\lor}=\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(F))
is also slope semistable with slope
-\beta
by propositon
A.3
. Thus,
F^{\lor}\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\leqslant-\beta}(X)
and
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))
is a subobject of
F^{\lor}
in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
. Therefore,
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))[-1]\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)[-2]
. Combine the filtration (
A.1
), we know that for any
l\leqslant 1
, we have
\mathcal{H}^{l}_{-\beta}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=0
.
∎

Now, suppose that
E\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
with
v_{1}^{\beta}(E)>0
, then we have a decomposition in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to E_{t}\to E\to E_{tf}\to 0,

where
E_{tf}\neq 0
is tilt-torsionfree and
v_{1}^{\beta}(E_{t})=0
. Note that
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{-\beta}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\cong\mathcal{H}^{1}_{-\beta}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E_{tf}))=E_{tf}^{\lor_{\beta}}
by the above lemma. In this case, we define the tilt-dual object of
E
as
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{-\beta}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)
and denote it by
E^{\lor_{\beta}}
.

Lemma A.9
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and suppose that
0\neq E\in\mathrm{Coh}^{\beta}_{0}(X)
is tilt-torsionfree, then
E^{\lor_{\beta}}\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)
is also tilt-torsionfree.

Proof.

If
E^{\lor_{\beta}}
is not tilt-torsionfree, then we have a nonzero subobject
A
of
E^{\lor_{\beta}}
in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)
with
v_{1}^{\beta}(A)=0
.
If
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(A)\neq 0
, then on one hand,
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(A)\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
is a slope semistable sheaf with slope
-\beta
. On the other hand
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(A)
is a subsheaf of
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})\cong\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\in\mathrm{Coh}^{<-\beta}_{0}(X)
by Lemma
A.7
and it is a contradiction, Thus
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(A)=0
and
A
is a nonzero sheaf with
\dim A\leqslant n-2
.

By (
14
), we have a distinguished triangle here:

\displaystyle E^{\lor_{\beta}}\to\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)[1]\to E^{\prime}[1]\to E^{\lor_{\beta}}[1]

and
\mathcal{H}^{l}(E^{\prime})\cong\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)
when
1<l<n
thus

\displaystyle E^{\prime}[1]\in\langle\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)[-1],\ldots,\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)[-1+n]\rangle.

Then we have:

\displaystyle 0\neq\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{0}^{b}(X)}(A,E^{\lor_{\beta}})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{0}^{b}(X)}(A,\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)[1])

by taking Hom functor. However,
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{0}^{b}(X)}(A,\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)[1])\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{0}^{b}(X)}(E,\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(A)[1])
.
Because
\dim A\leqslant n-2
, then
\mathcal{H}^{l}_{-\beta}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(A))\cong\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(A))
for any
l\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
and thus

\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(A)\in\langle\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)[-2],\ldots,\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)[-n]\rangle
. Thus
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{0}^{b}(X)}(A,\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)[1])=0
and it is a contradiction.
∎

Lemma A.10
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and
E\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
with
v_{1}^{\beta}(E)>0
, then
v_{1}^{-\beta}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})=v_{1}^{\beta}(E)>0
and
\nu^{-\beta,\alpha}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})\geqslant-\nu^{\beta,\alpha}(E)
.

Proof.

First of all, we assume that
E
is tilt-torsionfree. Then by Lemma
A.6
, we know that for any
l\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
,
\dim\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\leqslant n-l\leqslant n-2
. Thus we have

\displaystyle v_{2}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=v_{2}(\mathcal{H}^{2}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)))-v_{2}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})\geqslant-v_{2}(E^{\lor_{\beta}});

\displaystyle v_{1}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=-v_{1}(E^{\lor_{\beta}});

\displaystyle v_{0}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=-v_{0}(E^{\lor_{\beta}}).

by (
14
). Meanwhile, we know that
v_{i}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))=(-1)^{i}v_{i}(E)
for
i\in\{0,1,2\}
. Then

\displaystyle v_{1}^{-\beta}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})=v_{1}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})+\beta v_{0}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})=v_{1}(E)-\beta v_{0}(E)=v_{1}^{\beta}(E)>0

and

\displaystyle v_{2}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})-\alpha v_{0}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})\geqslant-v_{2}(E)+\alpha v_{0}(E)

Thus
\nu^{-\beta,\alpha}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})\geqslant-\nu^{\beta,\alpha}(E)
.

If
E
is not tilt-torsionfree, then we have a decomposition in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to E_{t}\to E\to E_{tf}\to 0,

where
E_{tf}\neq 0
is tilt-torsionfree and
v_{1}^{\beta}(E_{t})=0
. Then

\displaystyle v_{1}^{-\beta}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})=v_{1}^{-\beta}(E_{tf}^{\lor_{\beta}})=v_{1}^{\beta}(E_{tf})=v_{1}^{\beta}(E).

and

\displaystyle\nu^{-\beta,\alpha}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})=\nu^{-\beta,\alpha}(E_{tf}^{\lor_{\beta}})\geqslant-\nu^{\beta,\alpha}(E_{tf})=-\frac{(v_{2}(E)-\alpha v_{0}(E))-(v_{2}(E_{t})-\alpha v_{0}(E_{t}))}{v_{1}(E)-\beta v_{0}(E)}\geqslant-\nu^{\beta,\alpha}(E)

because
v_{1}^{\beta}(E_{t})=0
and
v_{2}(E_{t})-\alpha v_{0}(E_{t})\geqslant 0
.
∎

Remark A.11
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and suppose that
0\neq E\in\mathrm{Coh}^{\beta}_{0}(X)
is tilt-torsionfree, we expect the following property below: For any
1<l<n
,
\dim\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))\leqslant n-l-1
. If this conjecture holds, then we have
\nu^{-\beta,\alpha}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})=-\nu^{\beta,\alpha}(E)
.

If this conjecture holds, then we can see the concept of tilt-torsionfree is a variation of pureness. In fact, when
n=2
and
n=3
, the conjecture holds. But we still don’t know the consequence when
n\geqslant 4
.

If
n=2
, then
\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)\cong E^{\lor_{\beta}}[-1]
for any tilt-torsionfree object
E\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
. Then
E\cong\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})[1]
and by definition
E^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}\cong\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\beta}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E^{\lor_{\beta}}))\cong E
.

If
n=3
and
0\neq E\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
is a tilt-torsionfree object, then we have a distinguished triangle:

(15)

\displaystyle E^{\lor_{\beta}}[-1]\to\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E)\to\mathcal{H}^{2}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))[-2]\to E^{\lor_{\beta}},

where
\mathcal{H}^{2}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E))
is a zero dimensional sheaf.

Proposition A.12
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and
0\neq E\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
is tilt-torsionfree. If
\dim Y=3
, then:

(1) We have a short exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to E\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{E}}}{{\longrightarrow}}E^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}\to E^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}/E\to 0,

where
E^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}/E
is
0
or
\dim E^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}/E=0
.

(2) If
E
is
\nu^{\beta,\alpha}
-semistable, then
E^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}
is
\nu^{\beta,\alpha}
-semistable.

(3) If
E
is
\nu^{\beta,\alpha}
-semistable, then
E^{\lor_{\beta}}
is
\nu^{-\beta,\alpha}
-semistable with tilt-slope
-\nu^{\beta,\alpha}(E)
.

(4) Moreover, suppose that there is a short exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\longrightarrow}}E\to G\to 0

with
F\neq 0
and
G
is
0
or
\dim G=0
. Then there is a commutative diagram in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{F}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{F^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{E}}
\textstyle{E^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}}

with
f^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

(1) After taking relative dual functor of (
15
), we have a distinguished triangle in
D_{0}^{b}(X)
:

\displaystyle E^{\prime}[-1]\to E\to\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})[1]\to E^{\prime},

where
E^{\prime}
is a zero dimensional sheaf or
0
. Then we take the cohomology functor
\mathcal{H}_{\beta}^{l}
on the above sequence, and there is an exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to E\to E^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}\to E^{\prime}\to 0

and we get the conclusion.

(2) It is a direct conclusion of Lemma
A.2
and (1).

(3) Suppose that
E^{\lor_{\beta}}
is not
\nu^{-\beta,\alpha}
-semistable, then there is a short exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to A\to E^{\lor_{\beta}}\to B\to 0

with
+\infty>\nu^{-\beta,\alpha}(A)>\nu^{-\beta,\alpha}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})>\nu^{-\beta,\alpha}(B)
and
A,B\neq 0
since
E^{\lor_{\beta}}
is tilt-torsionfree. After taking the relative dual functor and the cohomology functor, we have an exact sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\to B^{\lor_{-\beta}}\to E^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}\to A^{\lor_{-\beta}}

Now
E^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}
is
\nu^{\beta,\alpha}
-semistable by (2) and
B^{\lor_{-\beta}}
is a nonzero subobject of
E^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}
. Thus

\displaystyle-\nu^{-\beta,\alpha}(B)\leqslant\nu^{\beta,\alpha}(B^{\lor_{-\beta}})\leqslant\nu^{\beta,\alpha}(E^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}})=-\nu^{-\beta,\alpha}(E^{\lor_{\beta}})

and this is a contradiction.

(4) It is not hard to see that
\iota_{E}\circ f=f^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}\circ\iota_{F}
. By taking the functor
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{-\beta}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(-))
, we know that
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{-\beta}(\mathbb{D}_{X/Y}(G))=0
. It implies
f^{\lor}
is an isomorphism and
f^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}
is also an isomorphism.
∎

A.2.
Some applications

We will give the proof about Notherian property of some tilting hearts. But before that, we will introduce some basic techniques about it.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a triangulated category. Suppose that
\sigma=(Z,\mathcal{A})
is a weak pre-stability condition on
\mathcal{D}
and
\mu
is the corresponding slope function. For any
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, let

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\beta}=\langle E\in\mathcal{A}\mid E\mbox{ is }\sigma\mbox{-semistable with }\mu(E)>\beta\rangle,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\beta}=\langle E\in\mathcal{A}\mid E\mbox{ is }\sigma\mbox{-semistable with }\mu(E)\leqslant\beta\rangle.

Then
(\mathcal{T}^{\beta},\mathcal{F}^{\beta})
is a torsion pair of
\mathcal{A}
by the existence of Harder-Narasimhan filtration. Let
\mathcal{A}^{\beta}=\langle\mathcal{F}^{\beta}[1],\mathcal{T}^{\beta}\rangle
, then it is a bounded heart of
\mathcal{D}
.

Proposition A.13
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and we assume that:

(1) the set
\{-\mathrm{Re}Z(E)-\beta\mathrm{Im}Z(E)\in\mathbb{R}\mid E\in\mathcal{A}^{\beta}\}
is discrete in
\mathbb{R}
;

(2) the abelian category
\mathcal{A}
is Noetherian;

(3) there is no infinite sequence in
\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle 0\neq G_{1}\subseteq G_{2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq G_{n}\subseteq G_{n+1}\subseteq\cdots

with
G_{n}\in\mathcal{F}^{\beta}
,
0\neq G_{n+1}/G_{n}\in\mathcal{C}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. Then the abelian category
\mathcal{A}^{\beta}
is Noetherian.

Proof.

Suppose that there is an infinite sequence of epimorphisms in
\mathcal{A}^{\beta}
:

\displaystyle E_{0}\twoheadrightarrow E_{1}\twoheadrightarrow E_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\cdots\twoheadrightarrow E_{n}\twoheadrightarrow E_{n+1}\twoheadrightarrow\cdots.

Then we have

\displaystyle-\operatorname{Re}Z(E_{0})-\beta\operatorname{Im}Z(E_{0})\geqslant-\operatorname{Re}Z(E_{1})-\beta\operatorname{Im}Z(E_{1})\geqslant-\operatorname{Re}Z(E_{2})-\beta\operatorname{Im}Z(E_{2})\geqslant\cdots\geqslant 0.

However,
\{-\operatorname{Re}Z(E)-\beta\operatorname{Im}Z(E)\in\mathbb{R}\mid E\in\mathcal{A}^{\beta}\}
is discrete in
\mathbb{R}
. Thus, when
n\gg 0
, we have
-\operatorname{Re}Z(E_{n})-\beta\operatorname{Im}Z(E_{n})=-\operatorname{Re}Z(E_{n+1})-\beta\operatorname{Im}Z(E_{n+1})=\cdots
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
-\operatorname{Re}Z(E_{n})-\beta\operatorname{Im}Z(E_{n})
is constant.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
E_{n}\neq E_{n+1}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and give a contradiction of it.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, let
F_{n}=\ker(E_{0}\to E_{n})\in\mathcal{A}^{\beta}
, then there is an infinite sequence in
\mathcal{A}^{\beta}
:

\displaystyle F_{1}\subseteq F_{2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq E_{0}.

By using the snake lemma, we have
\ker(E_{n}\to E_{n+1})\cong F_{n+1}/F_{n}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
in
\mathcal{A}^{\beta}
. And we note that
\operatorname{Re}Z(F_{n})+\beta\operatorname{Im}Z(F_{n})=0
. Because
E_{n}\neq E_{n+1}
, then
F_{n}\subsetneq F_{n+1}
.

After taking the cohomology functor with respect to the bounded heart
\mathcal{A}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{-1}(F_{1})\subseteq\mathcal{H}^{-1}(F_{2})\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{H}^{-1}(F_{n})\subseteq\mathcal{H}^{-1}(F_{n+1})\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{H}^{-1}(E_{0}).

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{0}(E_{0})\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{0}(E_{1})\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{0}(E_{2})\twoheadrightarrow\cdots\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{0}(E_{n})\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{0}(E_{n+1})\twoheadrightarrow\cdots.

Becasue
\mathcal{A}
is Noetherian, these two filtrations must be stable. Thus we assume that
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(F_{n})\cong\mathcal{H}^{-1}(F_{n+1})\subseteq\mathcal{H}^{-1}(E_{0})
and
\mathcal{H}^{0}(E_{n-1})\cong\mathcal{H}^{0}(E_{n})
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. Let
Q=\mathcal{H}^{-1}(E_{0})/\mathcal{H}^{-1}(F_{n})
.

Now, we will take the cohomology functor
\mathcal{H}^{l}
with respect to
\mathcal{A}
on the following short exact sequences in
\mathcal{A}^{\beta}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
:

\displaystyle 0\to F_{n}\to E_{0}\to E_{n}\to 0,

\displaystyle 0\to F_{n}\to F_{n+1}\to F_{n+1}/F_{n}\to 0,

\displaystyle 0\to F_{n+1}/F_{n}\to E_{n}\to E_{n+1}\to 0.

Then we get exact sequences in
\mathcal{A}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
:

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{H}^{-1}(F_{n})\to\mathcal{H}^{-1}(E_{0})\to\mathcal{H}^{-1}(E_{n})\to\mathcal{H}^{0}(F_{n})\to 0,

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{H}^{-1}(F_{n+1}/F_{n})\to\mathcal{H}^{0}(F_{n})\to\mathcal{H}^{0}(F_{n+1})\to\mathcal{H}^{0}(F_{n+1}/F_{n})\to 0,

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{H}^{-1}(F_{n+1}/F_{n})\to\mathcal{H}^{-1}(E_{n})\to\mathcal{H}^{-1}(E_{n+1})\to\mathcal{H}^{0}(F_{n+1}/F_{n})\to 0.

Because
\operatorname{Re}Z(F_{n})+\beta\operatorname{Im}Z(F_{n})=0
, then
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(F_{n})
is a
\sigma
-semistable object with slope
\beta
or
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(F_{n})=0
and
\mathcal{H}^{0}(F_{n})\in\mathcal{C}
by Lemma
. By the second exact sequence, the obejct
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(F_{n+1}/F_{n})
also need belong to
\mathcal{C}
, and obviously
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(F_{n+1}/F_{n})\in\mathcal{F}^{\beta}
. Then
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(F_{n+1}/F_{n})=0
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. Meanwhile,
\mathcal{H}^{0}(F_{n+1}/F_{n})
is a quotient object of
\mathcal{H}^{0}(F_{n+1})
and then
0\neq F_{n+1}/F_{n}\cong\mathcal{H}^{0}(F_{n+1}/F_{n})\in\mathcal{C}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
.

We also have a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{A}
as follows:

\displaystyle 0\to Q\to\mathcal{H}^{-1}(E_{n})\to\mathcal{H}^{0}(F_{n})\to 0.

If
Q=0
, it means for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
,
\mathcal{H}^{0}(F_{n})\cong\mathcal{H}^{-1}(E_{n})=0
and it will be contradiction because
0\neq F_{n+1}/F_{n}\cong\mathcal{H}^{0}(F_{n+1}/F_{n})
. Thus,
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(E_{n})\neq 0
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
.

Finally, let
G_{n}=\mathcal{H}^{-1}(E_{n})\in\mathcal{F}^{\beta}
,then we will have an infinite sequence in
\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle 0\neq G_{1}\subseteq G_{2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq G_{n}\subseteq G_{n+1}\subseteq\cdots

with
G_{n}\in\mathcal{F}^{\beta}
, and
0\neq F_{n+1}/F_{n}\cong G_{n+1}/G_{n}\in\mathcal{C}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. It is a contradiction with our condition on
\mathcal{A}
. Thus,
E_{n}=E_{n+1}
for
n\gg 0
.
∎

Proposition A.14
.

For any
\beta\in\mathbb{Q}
, the abelian category
\mathrm{Coh}^{\beta}_{0}(X)
is Noetherian.

Proof.

By Proposition
A.13
, we only need to prove the following: there is no infinite sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle 0\neq G_{1}\subseteq G_{2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq G_{m}\subseteq G_{m+1}\subseteq\cdots

with
G_{m}\in\mathrm{Coh}^{\leqslant\beta}_{0}(X)
, and the sheaf
G_{m+1}/G_{m}
is nonzero for any
m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
with
\dim G_{m+1}/G_{m}\leqslant n-2
. Note that the sheaf
G_{m}
is pure of dimension
n
.

We denote the cokernel of
G_{1}\to G_{m}
by
T_{m}
. We have another infinite sequence:

(16)

\displaystyle 0=T_{1}\subseteq T_{2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq T_{m}\subseteq T_{m+1}\subseteq\cdots

with
T_{m+1}/T_{m}\cong G_{m+1}/G_{m}\neq 0
for any
m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and
\dim T_{m}\leqslant n-2
by the snake lemma. By Lemma
A.3
, we have injections
T_{m}\to G_{1}^{\lor\lor}/G_{1}
in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
. By the Noetherian property of
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}(X)
,
T_{m}=T_{m+1}
for
m\gg 0
. and thus the sequence (
16
) must be stable and it is a contradiction.
∎

Proposition A.15
.

For any
(\beta,\alpha)\in U\cap\mathbb{Q}^{2}
, the abelian category
\mathrm{Coh}^{\beta,\alpha}_{0}(X)
is Noetherian.

Proof.

By proposition
A.13
, we only need to prove the following: there is no infinite sequence in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
for any
\beta
:

\displaystyle 0\neq G_{1}\subseteq G_{2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq G_{m}\subseteq G_{m+1}\subseteq\cdots

with
G_{m}\in\mathrm{Coh}^{\leqslant\beta,\alpha}_{0}(X)
, and the object
G_{m+1}/G_{m}
is a nonzero sheaf for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
with
\dim G_{m+1}/G_{m}=0
. Note that the object
G_{m}
is tilt-torsionfree.

We denote the cokernel of
G_{1}\to G_{m}
by
T_{m}
. We have another infinite sequence:

(17)

\displaystyle 0=T_{1}\subseteq T_{2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq T_{m}\subseteq T_{m+1}\subseteq\cdots

with
T_{m+1}/T_{m}\cong G_{m+1}/G_{m}\neq 0
and
T_{m}
is a nonzero dimensional sheaf by the snake lemma for any
m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. By Lemma
A.12
, we have injections
T_{m}\to G_{1}^{\lor_{\beta}\lor_{-\beta}}/G_{1}
in
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
. By the Noetherian property of
\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{\beta}(X)
, we have
T_{m}=T_{m+1}
for
m\gg 0
. and thus the sequence (
17
) must be stable and it is a contradiction.
∎

Proposition A.16
.

Suppose that
\dim Y=3
and
(\beta,\alpha)\in U
satisfies the Bogomolov-Gieseker type inequality. Then
(-\beta,\alpha)\in U
also satisfies the Bogomolov-Gieseker type inequality.

Proof.

Assume that there exists
\nu^{-\beta,\alpha}
-semistable object
E\in\mathrm{Coh}_{0}^{-\beta}(X)
with tilt-slope
-\beta
, which does not satisfy the Bogomolov-Gieseker type inequality, in other words,
v_{3}^{-\beta}(E)>\displaystyle\frac{2\alpha-\beta^{2}}{6}v_{1}^{-\beta}(E)
. However, by
A.12
,
E^{\lor_{-\beta}}
is a
\nu^{\beta,\alpha}
-semistable object with tilt-slope
\beta
.
Then

\displaystyle v_{3}^{\beta}(E^{\lor_{-\beta}})\leqslant\displaystyle\frac{2\alpha-\beta^{2}}{6}v_{1}^{\beta}(E^{\lor_{-\beta}}).

We can verify that
v_{3}^{-\beta}(E)\leqslant v_{3}^{\beta}(E^{\lor_{-\beta}})
and
v_{1}^{\beta}(E^{\lor_{-\beta}})=v_{1}^{-\beta}(E)
. Then we have a contradiction.
∎
